#### FAQ's - Prosperty Real Estate Chennai

##### How do I choose the right real estate consultant in Chennai?
To select the right consultant, consider their experience, reputation, client testimonials, and knowledge of the Chennai real estate market. It's essential to meet with them and discuss your specific needs and expectations.

##### What services can I expect from a real estate consultant in Chennai?
Real estate consultants offer a range of services, including property search and acquisition, property listing and marketing, negotiations, legal assistance, and market analysis. They can also help with property management and investment strategies.

##### What are the advantages of hiring a real estate advisor in Chennai?
Real estate advisors provide valuable insights into the local property market, helping you make informed decisions. They can save you time, negotiate better deals, and ensure legal compliance during property transactions.

##### What is property management, and why is it important in Chennai's real estate market?
Property management involves overseeing and maintaining properties on behalf of property owners. In Chennai's real estate market, it's crucial because it ensures that properties are well-maintained, tenants are managed, and rental income is optimized.

##### How can I find reliable property management services in Chennai?
Look for property management companies with a good track record, transparent fee structures, and references from satisfied clients. You can also inquire about their approach to property maintenance and tenant management.

##### Do I need a real estate agent to buy or sell property in Chennai?
While it's not mandatory, having a real estate agent can be highly beneficial. They have local market knowledge, negotiate on your behalf, and ensure a smooth transaction, especially if you're unfamiliar with Chennai's real estate market.

##### What does a real estate agent in Chennai do?
A real estate agent helps clients buy, sell, or rent properties. They act as intermediaries, showing properties, negotiating deals, and guiding clients through the entire transaction process.

##### What should I consider when hiring a real estate agent in Chennai?
Consider their experience, reputation, track record, and communication skills. It's also important to discuss your expectations, property preferences, and budget with them to ensure a successful collaboration.

## Benefits of an Exclusive Property Selling Mandate with Prosperty Real Estate Agents

### Experience the Benefits of an Exclusive Property Selling Mandate with Prosperty Real Estate Agents and Advisors
An exclusive property selling mandate is a contractual agreement between a property owner and a real estate agent. This contract gives the chosen agent exclusive rights to market and sell the property for a specified duration. This exclusivity offers significant advantages to property owners, as outlined below. Here at Prosperty Real Estate Agents and Advisors , we endorse this approach wholeheartedly and here’s why:

###### 1. Dedicated Service and Focus
With an exclusive mandate, we pledge our full time, focus, and resources to sell your property. The exclusivity guarantees that we are the only ones working on your sale, making us highly motivated to succeed.

###### 2. Streamlined and Efficient Communication
An exclusive mandate means all communication regarding your sale is centralized. This single communication channel avoids potential confusion and ensures efficient, transparent dialogue.

###### 3. Consistent Marketing and Branding
Your property deserves a comprehensive and consistent marketing strategy. With an exclusive mandate, we maintain a uniform brand voice and presentation, enhancing your property’s appeal to potential buyers.

###### 4. Accurate and Competitive Pricing
Our exclusive mandate allows us to understand your property deeply. This allows us to set an accurate, competitive price that truly reflects its value.

###### 5. Protection from Undercutting
When multiple agents are involved, they may be competing with each other by undercutting prices. With an exclusive mandate, we ensure that your property’s value is always upheld.

###### 6. Skilled Negotiation
At Prosperty Real Estate Agents and Advisors, we commit to thoroughly understanding every facet of your property. This equips us to effectively negotiate with potential buyers, securing you the best possible deal.

###### 7. Comprehensive Handling of Legalities and Paperwork
With an exclusive mandate, we take responsibility for all legalities and paperwork. Our meticulous approach ensures no detail is overlooked.

###### 8. Qualified Buyers Only
We spend time finding genuine, high-quality buyers, rather than rushing to make a quick sale. This guarantees that your property gets the right exposure to the right people.

###### 9. Complete Sales Process Management
We manage the entire process from staging and marketing to negotiation and closing the deal. With an exclusive mandate, you can relax knowing that professionals handle all aspects of the sale.

###### 10. Constructive Feedback and Improvement
As your exclusive real estate agent, we provide constructive feedback and suggest improvements after viewings. This valuable advice can help increase your property’s attractiveness and accelerate the sale.
Trust in the Prosperty Real Estate Agents and Advisors for your exclusive property selling mandate and enjoy our comprehensive service, strategic approach, and professional handling. Reach out to us today for a seamless and rewarding property sale experience.

#### Navigate Chennai Real Estate with Prosperty: A Guide to Buying Property in Chennai
Buying a property can be an overwhelming process, regardless of whether you’re a first-time buyer or a seasoned investor. At Prosperty Real Estate Advisors, we are committed to guiding you through every step of buying a property in Chennai. Over the last decade, we have been assisting our clients in making informed decisions while acquiring various assets. Let us show you how we make the process of purchasing a property easier, transparent, and tailored to your needs.

##### 1. Understanding Your Needs: The First Step to Buy Property in Chennai
Before you begin your search, it is crucial to identify your goals and objectives. Prosperty helps you define your requirements clearly, which in turn aids in focusing the property search. Whether you’re buying for yourself, as an investment, or for rental income, knowing your ‘why’ can significantly narrow down the property choices.

##### 2. Loan Assistance: Financing Your Property in Chennai
Every buyer wants their property acquisition to make financial sense. That’s why we offer personalised advice and guidance on financial planning. We help you understand your budget, consider the potential EMI obligations, and navigate the loan application process, ensuring that your property-buying journey is as smooth as possible.

##### 3. Choosing the Right Neighbourhood: Your Location Matters
Once you’ve identified your budget and requirements, we help you select the ideal neighbourhoods to consider. From public transport accessibility to nearby schools, we help you find a location that fits your lifestyle and meets your daily needs.

##### 4. Comprehensive Home Search: Find Your Perfect Property in Chennai
Sifting through properties can be time-consuming and tedious. We ease this process by providing a curated list of properties that meet your criteria. In addition, we ensure that the property has clear title deeds, necessary approvals, and is from a reputable developer.

##### 5. Making an Offer: Securing Your Ideal Property
After comparing and finalising your preferred property, we help you make a reasonable offer. Leveraging our expertise and in-depth knowledge of the Chennai real estate market, we ensure that your offer is competitive and fair.

##### 6. Negotiating: Getting the Best Price for Your Property
Negotiations can be daunting. With our experienced real estate agents by your side, you’ll be confidently navigating these conversations. We strive to ensure both parties reach a mutually beneficial agreement.

##### 7. Pre-closing Preparations: Laying the Groundwork
Before closing, it is crucial to prepare all necessary documents and ensure everything is in order. We guide you through this process to avoid any potential legal complications.

##### 8. Closing the Deal: Safely Completing Your Property Purchase in Chennai
We assist you in carefully reviewing all closing documents, ensuring that all agreements are met and all documents are in place. This ensures a smooth, problem-free transfer of ownership.

##### 9. Post-closing Action Plan: After You Buy Property in Chennai
Once the keys to your dream property are in your hands, we offer property management solutions to assist you further. Whether you want to move in or rent it out, we’re here to make the transition easy.
Buying a property in Chennai doesn’t have to be daunting. With Prosperty Real Estate Advisors, you’ll find that our personalised service, guided by facts and professional expertise, makes the entire process manageable and fulfilling. Trust us to be with you every step of the way on this journey.

##### Understanding Repatriation
Repatriation, in essence, means the process of returning a person’s assets from a foreign country to their home country. For NRIs, this often pertains to the proceeds gained from the sale of property or real estate in India. However, there are regulatory hoops to jump through, and that’s where expert guidance becomes indispensable.

##### Why Repatriation Consultation Matters
Firstly, being aware of the rules is essential. The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has specific guidelines for repatriation, and they evolve over time. Staying updated ensures you aren’t caught off guard.
Moreover, taxes play a significant role. It’s not just about paying them, but understanding the exemptions, benefits, and optimal ways to structure the repatriation to minimize the tax hit.

##### Our Array of Repatriation Services
- Regulatory Updates and Compliance: We keep a vigilant eye on RBI guidelines, ensuring you’re always aligned with the latest regulations.
- Tax Consultation: From understanding Double Taxation Avoidance Agreements (DTAAs) to seeking potential tax exemptions, our experts guide you through the labyrinth of Indian tax norms.
- Documentation Assistance: Repatriation requires a slew of documents, from tax clearance certificates to bank statements. We assist in ensuring every paper is in order.
- Banking Guidance: Whether it’s opening an NRO (Non-Residential Ordinary) or NRE (Non-Residential External) account or liaising with banks for smooth repatriation, we’re by your side.

###### Repatriation Beyond Real Estate
While real estate often forms a substantial chunk of repatriation funds, other assets like fixed deposits or mutual funds can also be repatriated. We extend our expertise to these domains, ensuring a holistic repatriation experience.

##### Important Documents To Make A Note Of:
- Check for the appropriate conveyance of the title that is in favor of the developer. This ensures that the developer owns the land rightfully. Hiring professionals from Prosperty Real Estate Chennai is a win-win situation here.
- It is crucial to check whether the seller is the true owner of the property. The buyer should seek clear ownership history if the property has changed hands multiple times.
- Apart from the sale deed, other papers that are needed in the sub-registrar’s office are a letter from the society giving details about the number of floors in the building, the construction year, the apartment’s built-up area and the number of elevators, etc.
- Check the license or the development right and approvals of the builder. Don’t shy away from asking them. It is mandatory to show these documents to the customers.
- Make sure there is the proper execution of the Athe allotment Letter or Sale Agreement on your payments. You can also sit back and relax and let Prosperty Real Estate Agents in Chennai do the legwork.
- For those who all are unaware, an allotment letter is nothing but proof. It is a written proof that the unit or property you select is yours.
- On the other hand, the Sale Agreement is a legal document that ensures that the developer has agreed to sell the property to you.
- See the documents wherein reputed financial companies have approved the project. This will further help you in financing yourself.
- Check the tentative layout plan and keep it handy. In case there are changes later, you should pinpoint them.
- There is a slightly different process if a property is mortgaged. Then the owner gives an undertaking to the bank. He acknowledges that he has agreed to give property documents to the buyer upon the home loan foreclosure.
- Always ask for Occupation or Completion Certificate before paying the final installment. The municipal authorities issue these to the developers on completion of the project. It’s a piece of cake for the experts from Prosperty Real Estate Chennai .
- Also, keep an assessment bill handy. Bills from the local civic body, a copy of the property card, and a receipt for the payment of the registration fee. Keep xeroxes of all of these documents.
- Make sure you register the Conveyance Deed. Do this as soon as you make the entire payment. Only after that, the property now legally belongs to you. You can cross-check with Prosperty Real Estate Agents in Chennai .
In general, for buying a property you need to check the Deed of Conveyance, Mutation Certificate (for the entire property), Land Registration Status, Sanctioned Plan, Search Report, and Payment Schedule (for under- construction property).

##### Take It Easy
It is important to ensure that the seller produces all the relevant original documents. This is where the role of the registrar comes in. The registrar has the responsibility of establishing the authenticity of these documents. Apart from this, Prosperty Real Estate Agents in Chennai are extremely talented and experienced.
Other challenges that a buyer may face include the absence of a pa roper chain of documentation. This happens when the property has changed hands a couple of times in the past. If a property is thirty years old, there’s a possibility that it was never documented. So this is where the legal issues arise and you would need Prosperty Real Estate Chennai .

##### Why are Suburbs Preferred?
There are generally two types of people who prefer to buy in the suburbs. One is those people who are looking for spacious spaces. ##### What Is A Peripheral Area?
Peripheral areas are simply opposite of that of a suburb. They are pretty far from the core city areas. It is far away from all the hustle-bustle of the main city life. So peripheral areas form the fringes of the suburban areas.

##### What Are The Advantages Of Peripheral Area?

Real estate agents bring with them a wealth of expertise and market understanding that most owners don’t have. They know how to price a property properly, understand market trends, and can effectively negotiate on your behalf. #### Network & Exposure
Real estate agents have extensive networks that they leverage to find buyers. These networks often extend beyond what’s accessible to the general public, providing a wider audience for your listing. #### Time & Commitment

On the other hand, real estate agents dedicate their professional lives to these tasks, ensuring every aspect of the selling process is meticulously handled.

#### Trust & Credibility
Finally, buyers tend to trust agent listings more. They feel reassured knowing that a professional is involved, which decreases the chances of potential legal issues or scams.

The additional cost of hiring an agent is often offset by their benefits – a quicker sale, better price, and less stress for you, the seller. #### The Risks of Selling Your House Without an Agent: 10 Key Reasons to Rethink
Thinking about selling your house without an agent ? While it might seem tempting to save on commission, the complexities of a real estate transaction can be overwhelming. Here are 10 key reasons why hiring a professional agent can greatly benefit your property sale:

###### 1. Accurate Property Pricing
Pricing a property accurately requires in-depth market knowledge. An agent has the expertise to set a competitive price that reflects your property’s true value, preventing costly under or overpricing.

###### 2. Significant Time Commitment
Selling a property is a time-consuming process. Market research, property promotion, handling enquiries, and negotiations require a significant time investment, which can be taxing for busy homeowners.

###### 3. Limited Market Exposure
Without an agent, your property’s exposure could be limited. Agents can access broad networks of potential buyers, increasing the chances of a quick and profitable sale.

###### 4. Expert Negotiation Skills
Negotiating a sales price is complex and challenging. A skilled agent negotiates on your behalf, using their expertise to secure a deal in your best interest.

###### 5. Managing Contracts and Paperwork
Real estate transactions involve extensive legal contracts and paperwork. A small mistake could have serious consequences. Agents have the knowledge and experience to handle these complexities.

###### 6. Addressing Potential Setbacks
Numerous obstacles can arise during sales, from financing issues to property inspection problems. An agent knows how to navigate these issues, keeping the process moving smoothly.

###### 7. Property Presentation
Agents understand what potential buyers look for and can provide valuable advice on staging your property to maximize appeal.

###### 8. Handling Home Inspections
Home inspections can reveal issues that might be a deal-breaker for buyers. An agent can help address these issues effectively or negotiate terms to satisfy both parties.

###### 9. Emotional Aspect
Selling a property can be emotional. An agent provides an objective perspective and can handle interactions with buyers professionally, removing personal feelings from the process.

###### 10. Closing the Deal
Closing a real estate transaction involves multiple steps and meticulous attention to detail. An agent ensures everything is in order, leading to a seamless, successful close.
In short, while selling your property on your own might seem cost-effective initially, it often involves hidden costs in terms of time, stress, and potential legal risks. Hiring a real estate agent provides a smoother, faster sale, making it a sound investment. When considering selling your property, remember professional help is just a call away.

## Timing is Everything: When Should You Conduct a Property Legal Title Check

**URL:** https://www.prospertyrealestate.com/timing-is-everything-when-should-you-conduct-a-property-legal-title-check/  
**SEO title:** Timing is Everything: When Should You Conduct a Property Legal Title Check – PROSPERTY  
**Published:** 2023-05-21  
**Category:** Real Estate Buy  
**Words:** 417


In the world of real estate, a property legal title check is akin to a heart check-up before running a marathon. You wouldn’t want to risk it without knowing your health condition. Similarly, delving into a real estate investment without a property legal title check can pose significant risks. But when exactly should this crucial step take place? Let’s explore this important aspect.

##### Understanding a Property Legal Title Check
First, let’s quickly define what we mean by “property legal title check”. It’s an intensive investigation process into a property’s legal status, aiming to confirm the seller’s rightful ownership and uncover any potential issues that could affect the transfer of property rights to a new owner.

##### The Ideal Time for a Property Legal Title Check
One of the most common queries that we come across is about the timing of the property legal title check. When should it ideally be carried out? Here are three scenarios:

###### At the time of the Property Purchase :
The most common and crucial time to conduct a property legal title check is when you plan to purchase a property. It is essential to ensure that the seller is the rightful owner and there are no hidden legal troubles that could pop up after you’ve made the investment.

###### While Applying for a Home Loan :
Banks and financial institutions necessitate a property legal title check as part of their due diligence when you apply for a home loan. It’s their way of ensuring that the collateral against the loan (the property) is free from legal troubles. However, it’s recommended to conduct your independent title check even before approaching the bank.

###### Before Taking a Long-Term Commercial Lease :
If you’re considering taking a property on a long-term lease for commercial use, conducting a title check is crucial. The long-term nature of the contract means you’re locked into a legal agreement for an extended period. Ensuring the property is free from legal issues protects your business interests.

##### Conclusion:
A property legal title check is not a step you would want to skip or delay. Ensuring it is conducted at the right time is equally important. Consider it as the due diligence needed to protect your investment and avoid potential legal pitfalls. After all, buying a property often involves a significant chunk of your hard-earned savings. It’s worth spending a little extra time and effort to ensure it’s a sound investment.

## Your Comprehensive Guide to the House Construction Process

**URL:** https://www.prospertyrealestate.com/your-comprehensive-guide-to-the-house-construction-process/  
**SEO title:** Your Comprehensive Guide to the House Construction Process – PROSPERTY  
**Published:** 2023-05-21  
**Category:** General  
**Words:** 687


#### Your Comprehensive Guide to the House Construction Process
Crafting your dream home is more than just a construction project – it’s an intimate journey that reflects your personality, ambitions, and lifestyle. Understanding the intricate house construction process is paramount to transforming your vision into a tangible reality. In this comprehensive guide, we’ll navigate through every critical step in the house construction process, providing you with a roadmap to building your ideal home.

##### Understanding the House Construction Process
The house construction process can be broadly divided into two phases – the Pre-Construction phase and the Construction phase. Each phase is made up of several key stages that play a vital role in the formation of your home. Let’s delve into these stages to understand the entire process better.

##### The Pre-Construction Phase
The first step of the house construction process involves intensive planning and preparation. This phase includes the building plan, budget estimation, land acquisition, and documentation – all essential foundations for a successful project.
- Building Plan: Your house starts with a detailed plan. This blueprint dictates the layout and features of your home, from room sizes to architectural design. Professionals like architects, civil engineers, and interior designers work together to create a comprehensive building plan that aligns with your vision.
- Budget Estimation: Building a house requires a considerable financial commitment. After finalizing your building plan, a building estimator can help you estimate the cost of materials, machinery, and labor needed to bring your dream home to life. This budget estimation will guide your financial planning and help prevent any unexpected costs.
- Land Acquisition: The next crucial stage in the house construction process is choosing a suitable plot of land. The land’s location, size, and cost will greatly impact your construction project. When deciding, make sure to factor in the size of your desired house, the number of rooms, and your preferred location.
- Documentation: Proper documentation is vital to avoid legal complications during the house construction process. Before you start building, ensure you have obtained the necessary permissions from relevant authorities and all your ownership documents are up-to-date.

##### The Construction Phase
With planning and preparation complete, you’re ready to begin the physical construction. This phase is the heart of the house construction process, where your dream home starts to take shape.
- Site Clearing: The first step of construction involves preparing the land for building. This means clearing the site of any obstacles, such as trees, plants, and debris. Professionals equipped with machinery, like bulldozers, undertake this task.
- Laying the Foundation: The foundation acts as the backbone of your home, providing the structural support needed for the entire building. A strong foundation is critical for the stability and longevity of your house.
- Plinth Beam and Slab: After laying the foundation, a plinth beam and slab are added to enhance its strength and protect it from potential damage.
- Superstructure: The superstructure refers to the part of the building above the foundation. This includes the walls, columns, and beams that provide additional support for the building.
- Bricklaying: The walls of your home are constructed during the bricklaying stage. Depending on your preference, you can choose between bricks or concrete blocks.
- The Lintel and Roof Coating: Once the walls are erected, lintels are constructed above doors and windows to support the structure. The roof is installed to protect the building from external elements.
- Plumbing and Electrical Wiring: Plumbing and electrical systems are crucial for modern houses. These systems are usually installed during construction to ensure they are seamlessly integrated into the building.
- Exterior and Interior Designing: With the main structure completed, the focus shifts to exterior and interior designing. Plastering the exterior walls gives the house a smooth finish, while interior designing brings the internal spaces to life.

###### Conclusion
Building your dream home is a significant and fulfilling journey. Every step in the house construction process requires careful planning, attention to detail, and a collaborative effort from various professionals. By understanding each phase, you can ensure that your home is built to your exact specifications, providing a comfortable and personalized space for you and your loved ones.

## Advantages of Buying a Resale flat in Chennai

**URL:** https://www.prospertyrealestate.com/advantages-of-buying-a-resale-flat-in-chennai/  
**SEO title:** Advantages of Buying a Resale flat in Chennai – PROSPERTY  
**Published:** 2023-09-14  
**Category:** Real Estate Buy  
**Words:** 343


#### The Benefits of Buying a Resale Flat in Chennai
Buying a resale flat in Chennai is a pivotal decision with many advantages. For potential homeowners, this choice often stands out in the property market. From being move-in ready to sometimes being more affordable, resale flats present numerous benefits. If you’re considering buying a resale flat in Chennai, here’s why it might just be the perfect decision for you:
- Immediate Possession: Buying a resale apartment in Chennai comes with significant perks. One primary advantage is moving in immediately, bypassing construction delays.
- Established Neighborhood: Resale flats in Chennai are typically situated in well-established areas. This guarantees robust infrastructure, reliable utilities, and enhanced connectivity.
- Transparent Transactions: In most cases, previous owners have all the necessary documentation in place, which reduces the risk of ownership disputes or legal issues.
- Cost-Effective: Sometimes, buying a resale flat in Chennai can be more economical than new properties, especially if the previous owner is looking for a quick sale.
- Minimal GST Impact: Unlike new properties where you may need to pay GST, resale properties often have a different tax implication. The impact of taxes can be much lower or even nil when purchasing these properties
- Tangible Assessment: You can inspect the actual condition of the property firsthand, ensuring you know precisely what you’re investing in.
- Negotiation Opportunities: In many cases, there’s a possibility to negotiate the price directly with the seller. This allows for potentially better deals.
- Matured Community: Older buildings often have a settled resident community. This can provide a more cohesive and harmonious living experience.
- No Pre-EMI: For those availing loans, buying a resale apartment in Chennai offers a distinct advantage. There are no Pre-EMIs, which are typically applicable for under-construction properties.
- Customizations: Many resale flats come with interior modifications and customizations already in place, saving you time and effort.
In conclusion, buying a resale flat in Chennai offers numerous benefits. For first-time buyers or investors, it promises cost-effectiveness. Always consider these advantages when making your choice.

## Buying an Apartment? Avoid These Common Mistakes

**URL:** https://www.prospertyrealestate.com/buying-an-apartment-avoid-these-common-mistakes/  
**SEO title:** Buying an Apartment? Avoid These Common Mistakes – PROSPERTY  
**Published:** 2023-09-14  
**Category:** Real Estate Buy  
**Words:** 372


#### Common Mistakes to Avoid When Buying an Apartment
Embarking on the journey of buying an apartment? While the path is filled with anticipation, it’s also fraught with potential missteps. Here’s a guide to ensure your apartment-buying experience is smooth.

###### 1 . Overlooking the Budget:
When buying an apartment, it’s crucial to set a clear budget. Venturing without one can lead to unnecessary financial strain.

###### 2 . Missing Out on the Fine Print:
Contracts associated with purchasing an apartment are exhaustive. Bypassing the small details today can spell trouble tomorrow.

###### 3 . Getting Swayed by the Model Apartment:
Model apartments are meant to captivate. What you see might not be what you get. Always verify specifics.

###### 4. Forgetting Maintenance Estimates:
The costs of purchasing an apartment don’t end at its price. Regular maintenance and society fees add up over time.

###### 5. Not Investigating the Builder’s History:
Before buying an apartment, research the developer’s past projects. Ensure a legacy of timely delivery and quality.

###### 6. Overlooking Future Neighborhood Plans:
Before sealing the deal on purchasing an apartment, check for future development plans in the vicinity. It can influence your property’s future value.

###### 7. Bypassing a Professional Home Inspection:
A meticulous inspection is a must when buying an apartment. Hidden issues, if any, should be spotted early.

###### 8. Not Thinking About Resale Potential:
When purchasing an apartment, consider its long-term value. Will it be a sought-after property years down the line?

###### 9. Rushing the Buying Process:
Take your time. Buying an apartment is a significant decision. Don’t let seller pressure speed up your choice.

###### 10. Not Comparing Home Loan Rates:
The process of purchasing an apartment likely involves a loan. Different institutions offer varied rates; it pays to compare.

###### 11. Misunderstanding ‘Furnished’ Apartments:
Furnished doesn’t always mean move-in ready. Check the quality and utility of provided items when purchasing an apartment.

###### 12. Overlooking Parking Needs:
A good apartment in a bustling city loses its charm without parking. Ensure this essential is in place.

##### Conclusion:
The process of buying an apartment is both exhilarating and challenging. By being vigilant and sidestepping these common pitfalls, your journey to acquiring your dream home becomes all the more rewarding.



## Double-Check Before You Leap: The Importance of Multiple Home Inspections for Your Apartment Buy in Chennai

**URL:** https://www.prospertyrealestate.com/double-check-before-you-leap-the-importance-of-multiple-home-inspections-for-your-apartment-buy-in-chennai/  
**SEO title:** Double-Check Before You Leap: The Importance of Multiple Home Inspections for Your Apartment Buy in Chennai – PROSPERTY  
**Published:** 2023-09-14  
**Category:** Real Estate Buy  
**Words:** 371


#### Ensuring the Best Apartment Buy in Chennai: The Power of Multiple Home Inspections
So, you’ve shortlisted an apartment in Chennai and are almost ready to take the plunge. But wait! Have you done your due diligence? Multiple home inspections are a must, and here’s why.

##### Why Inspect More Than Once?
1. Lighting Conditions Vary: Visit once during the morning, then again in the afternoon or evening. Natural light can reveal flaws or aspects that artificial lighting might hide.
2. Meet Different Neighbours: Every visit offers a chance to meet and talk to different residents. They can provide unique insights about the community and potential issues.
3. Check for Consistency: Is that water stain a one-off occurrence or a sign of consistent leakage? Multiple checks can clarify.
4. Experience Different Days: Weekdays might be peaceful, but weekends could be noisy or vice-versa. It’s essential to know what to expect on different days.
5. Pressure Test the Facilities: During one visit, check the taps and showers for water pressure. On another, see how the electrical outlets fare with multiple appliances on.

##### The “Apartment Buy in Chennai” Checklist:
1. Structural Soundness: Look for cracks, dampness, or any sign of structural damage. Check once, check twice.
2. Water Issues: Chennai’s rain can be heavy. Ensure there’s no seepage, especially in the monsoon months.
3. Electricals and Fixtures: Flick those switches more than once. Ensure consistent functionality.
4. Safety: Multiple checks allow you to gauge the safety of the neighbourhood, the security measures in place, and the overall vibe of the area.
5. Amenities: If the apartment complex boasts amenities like a pool or gym, ensure they’re well-maintained. A second or third visit might show different usage patterns.

###### Pro Tip:
Always have a list during your visits. It ensures you don’t miss out on any aspect during your multiple inspections.

##### Conclusion:
While an apartment buy in Chennai is an exciting endeavour, it’s crucial to be thorough. Multiple home inspections might seem tedious, but they’re your best bet to ensure you’re making a wise, well-informed investment. After all, a home is a long-term commitment; it’s worth the extra time and effort!

##### Foundational Documents – The Starting Point of Your Purchase
- Sale Deed: The heart of your property transaction. It proves the sale and the transfer of ownership from the seller to you.
- Mother Deed: Think of this as the ancestry document of your flat. It traces back the original property ownership, establishing a clear history.

##### Ensuring a Clean Slate: Legal and Financial Clearances
- Encumbrance Certificate: Your assurance that the flat has no legal claims or liabilities. A clean slate is always best!
- Tax Receipts: Always double-check the latest receipts. This ensures the seller has settled all municipal dues, leaving no bills for you.

##### Building Details: Aligning with the List of Documents for Resale Flat
- Building Plan: Municipal authorities approve these. Ensure the flat’s construction matches this plan to avoid unwanted surprises.
- Occupancy Certificate: This one’s for completed buildings. The municipal stamp of approval, confirming the building’s fitness for occupation.
- Completion Certificate: For apartments, this is the builder’s promise that everything’s done by the book.

##### No Surprises: Ensuring a Smooth Transition
- No Objection Certificate (NOC): A crucial seal of approval from the housing society or apartment association. It says, “We’re okay with this sale.”
- Loan Release Letter: If the seller had a property loan, this document from the bank states it’s been cleared.
- Khata Certificate: A testament that the property tax is up-to-date with the local municipality or panchayat.

##### Title and Utilities: Sealing the Deal
- Title Deed: The bedrock that ensures the property’s title is crystal clear and free from disputes.
- Utility Bills: Recent bills like electricity and water indicate no pending dues. Always a good sign.
In conclusion, securing this list of documents for resale flat in Chennai is like piecing together a jigsaw puzzle. Each document fits perfectly, paving the way for a smooth purchase. Always consult with a legal expert to ensure you’re well-covered, and happy house hunting!



## Pros and Cons of Buying Resale Apartment in Chennai: An Insightful Guide

**URL:** https://www.prospertyrealestate.com/pros-and-cons-of-buying-resale-apartment-in-chennai-an-insightful-guide/

**Published:** 2023-09-14  
**Category:** Real Estate Buy  
**Words:** 434


#### The Two Sides of Buying Resale Apartment in Chennai
Considering a resale apartment for your next home in Chennai? Understanding the pros and cons of buying a resale apartment in Chennai can ensure you make an informed decision.

##### Pros of Buying a Resale Apartment in Chennai:
- Established Neighborhood: Resale apartments are typically in well-developed areas with established amenities and infrastructures.
- Immediate Possession: No waiting for construction to finish. You can move in as soon as the deal is sealed.
- Cost-Effective: Often, resale apartments are priced lower than brand-new counterparts, especially if they’re a few years old.
- Tried and Tested: Any major issues would likely have been identified and addressed by the previous owners.
- Negotiation Leeway: Buying directly from homeowners often allows more flexibility in pricing negotiations.
- Legal Clarity: Older properties in well-settled areas tend to have clearer titles and fewer legal disputes.
- Transparent History: Potential buyers can interact with neighbours to get insights into the apartment’s history, issues, and community living experiences.
- No GST: Resale homes don’t attract Goods and Services Tax (GST) which is applicable for under-construction properties.
- Matured Society: Established apartments often have well-set community rules, active associations, and a sense of camaraderie among residents.
- Location Advantage: Many resale properties are centrally located, giving better access to schools, hospitals, and other essentials.

##### Cons of Buying a Resale Apartment in Chennai:
- Older Infrastructure: Resale apartments may lack the modern facilities or aesthetic appeal of new buildings.
- Higher Maintenance: As buildings age, they can require more upkeep, leading to higher maintenance charges.
- Less Customization: Altering or renovating an existing apartment can be more challenging and costly.
- Loan Limitations: Financial institutions might be reluctant to finance older properties, limiting your borrowing amount.
- Less Energy Efficient: Older constructions may not be as energy-efficient, leading to higher utility bills.
- Past Legal Disputes: It’s crucial to ensure that the property has no pending legal disputes or issues.
- Wear and Tear: Natural ageing of the property can lead to wear and tear which might not be immediately evident.
- Outdated Design: The layout or design may not align with current architectural trends.
- Lack of Warranty: Unlike new homes that come with warranties on fittings and fixtures, resale homes offer no such guarantees.
- Potential Hidden Costs: There may be unexpected expenses in terms of repairs or replacements.

##### Conclusion:
Buying a resale apartment in Chennai has its advantages and disadvantages. A thorough evaluation, keeping the above points in mind, can help in making an informed decision. Always remember to engage in diligent checks and seek professional advice before making your investment.

#### Owner Listings vs Agent Listings: Understanding the Difference
The real estate market is buzzing with countless property listings. From glossy brochures to detailed online portals, the options seem endless. However, there’s an underlying difference between listings put up by owners and those by professional agents. Let’s delve into why owner listings often fall short when compared to agent listings.

###### 1. Professional Presentation:
Agent Advantage : Agents often have access to professional photographers and staging experts. This results in high-quality images that showcase the property in the best light.
Owner Challenge : Owners may rely on smartphone photos, which might not capture the essence of the property as effectively.

###### 2. Accurate Pricing:
Agent Advantage : Agents bring in-depth market knowledge. They ensure properties are priced competitively based on current trends and comprehensive market analyses.
Owner Challenge : Owners might price their property based on personal valuation, emotions, or outdated information, potentially leading to overpricing or underpricing.

###### 3. Effective Marketing:
Agent Advantage : Real estate agents have a broader reach. They can leverage multiple platforms, from social media to dedicated real estate portals, to market the property effectively.
Owner Challenge : An owner’s marketing is often limited to a few platforms, possibly missing out on a significant chunk of potential buyers.

###### 4. Time Commitment:
Agent Advantage : Agents take care of everything – from answering inquiries to scheduling viewings, ensuring a seamless process.
Owner Challenge : Owners, juggling their daily commitments, might not always be available, leading to missed opportunities.

###### 5. Negotiation Skills:
Agent Advantage : With vast experience, agents are adept at negotiating favourable terms and prices.
Owner Challenge : Negotiation can be daunting for owners, potentially leading to less favourable terms.

###### 6. Knowledge of Legalities:
Agent Advantage : Agents are well-versed with the legal aspects of property sales, ensuring all paperwork is accurate and complete.
Owner Challenge : Owners might overlook certain legal requirements, risking complications later on.

###### 7. Emotional Detachment:
Agent Advantage : An agent approaches a sale with detachment, focusing solely on the property’s market value and potential.
Owner Challenge : Owners can be emotionally attached to their properties, which might cloud their judgment during negotiations or while setting a price.

###### 8. Access to a Network:
Agent Advantage : Agents often have a vast network, including potential buyers, fellow agents, and industry professionals.
Owner Challenge : Owners might not have this extensive reach, limiting potential buyer interactions.

###### 9. Feedback Loop:
Agent Advantage : Agents receive constant feedback from potential buyers and other agents, allowing them to tweak the listing for better results.
Owner Challenge : Owners might not get the same level of actionable feedback.

###### Conclusion:
While owner listings come from a personal, passionate place, they often lack the professional touch, expansive reach, and market insight of agent listings. For those considering “Owner Listings vs Agent Listings”, it’s clear that professional agents bring a host of advantages to the table, ensuring a smoother, more profitable sale.



## The Art of Effective Real Estate Negotiation

**URL:** https://www.prospertyrealestate.com/the-art-of-effective-real-estate-negotiation/  
**SEO title:** The Art of Effective Real Estate Negotiation – PROSPERTY  
**Published:** 2023-09-17  
**Category:** Real Estate Buy  
**Words:** 610


#### Unlocking Chennai’s Real Estate: Negotiation Mastery
Navigating the real estate market, especially in bustling locations like Chennai, India, can be a challenging endeavor. One of the most critical aspects of this journey is mastering the art of negotiation. Effective real estate negotiation can be the difference between a favourable deal and an overpriced purchase. Let’s delve into the most effective negotiation tactics to ensure you’re well-prepared to secure the best deal for your dream apartment.

###### 1. Do Your Research:
Before diving into negotiations, arm yourself with thorough knowledge about the local real estate market. Understand the average price per square foot in your desired locality, recent sales, and the demand-supply dynamics. This research sets the foundation for all negotiation strategies, ensuring you know when to push for a lower price or when a deal is already optimal.

###### 2. Be Patient and Show Less Enthusiasm:
While it’s natural to be excited about a potential dream apartment, showing too much eagerness can weaken your negotiating position. Display a neutral interest, allowing you more leeway to discuss terms and pricing.

###### 3. Understand the Seller’s Motivation:
Every seller has a reason for listing their property. It could be urgency due to relocation, financial needs, or other personal reasons. Identifying this motivation can help tailor your negotiation strategy and potentially get a better deal.

###### 4. Use Time to Your Advantage:
Properties that have been on the market for an extended period might have more negotiable pricing. Sellers might be more willing to reduce the price if they’ve been trying to sell for months.

###### 5. Get Pre-Approved for a Loan:
Having a pre-approved mortgage not only shows sellers you’re serious but can also give you an upper hand in negotiations. It subtly communicates that while you’re keen, you also have other options available.

###### 6. Request for Home Inspection:
A thorough home inspection can reveal potential issues or repairs needed in the apartment. These findings can be powerful and effective real estate negotiation tactics, allowing you to either request a price reduction or ask the seller to handle the repairs.

###### 7. Be Open to Compromise:
Effective real estate negotiation isn’t about winning every point but finding a balance where both parties feel they’re getting a fair deal. Understand what’s most important to you (price, repair works, move-in date) and be ready to compromise on other aspects.

###### 8. Use a Skilled Real Estate Agent:
Employing an agent skilled in effective real estate negotiation can be invaluable. They bring experience, understand local market nuances, and can often secure better terms than individuals negotiating on their own.

###### 9. Highlight a Bulk Purchase or Immediate Closure:
If you’re considering purchasing multiple units or can guarantee a swift closure, use this as a negotiation chip. Sellers often favor quick transactions or the prospect of larger sales.

###### 10. Always Be Ready to Walk Away:
As counterintuitive as it may seem, one of the most effective tools in real estate negotiation is the ability to walk away. If terms aren’t aligning with your expectations and there’s little room for further negotiation, showing a willingness to explore other options can sometimes shift the dynamic in your favour.

##### Conclusion:
Negotiating an apartment purchase in Chennai’s vibrant real estate market requires a blend of research, patience, and strategic thinking. By mastering the art of effective real estate negotiation, you not only ensure a favourable deal but also step into your dream home with the satisfaction of a transaction well-negotiated. As always, the journey becomes significantly smoother with expert guidance and a clear understanding of market dynamics.

## A Will -and its characteristics

**URL:** https://www.prospertyrealestate.com/a-will-and-its-characteristics/  
**SEO title:** A Will -and its characteristics – PROSPERTY  
**Published:** 2025-10-17  
**Category:** General  
**Words:** 1262


#### A will is a confidential and secret document.
A Will is a legal declaration that a person intends to carry out regarding his property after death. A Will is only valid after death. Writing a Will is a traditional way to pass on everything a person has earned or inherited from his ancestors to the next generation.
Everyone who wishes his assets to pass to his heirs smoothly should make a will. It is a myth that Wills are only for the wealthy. So, Everyone should make a Will to ensure that their heirs have access to what is rightfully theirs.  hence,  Anyone can make a Will to distribute their assets. Assets can include property, gold, investments in financial instruments, art, and artefacts—even cash at home.
It is not necessary that the will should be registered or stamped.
You can write A Will on any paper.
It will still be legal as a Will prepared by a lawyer.
the will should identify you as the testator or the person making the Will, list your assets, and indicate the division among the beneficiaries. It doesn’t matter if you write it down or type it; as long as it is signed by the executor and witnessed by at least two witnesses, the Will will be considered legal.

#### Will Making
Will-making is a simple task that most people can do themselves. Only one requirement is that the Will must be legible. If your assets or ownership are complicated, you may need a lawyer to help you draft your Will. He would make sure that the language is clear and not ambiguous. This could prevent future disputes. He would also ensure that distributions are within the legal parameters.
To avoid disputes between his heirs, an elderly person should not write a Will as he will be too frail but rather have it typed. The natural succession of wealth inherited from the family cannot be overridden by a Will. No matter your financial situation, it is vital that you make a will.

#### Why is it Imperative to Have a Will?
Some rules govern how money, property, and possessions should be distributed if you die without a will. It may not be how you want your money or possessions to be distributed.
Partners who are not married and those who have not registered a civil partner will not be in a position to inherit from the other unless there is a Will. If one partner dies, it could cause serious financial problems for the other.
You will need to create a Will if you have children. This will allow you to make arrangements for your children in the event of either parent’s death.
If you seek advice in advance and make a Will, it may be possible to lower the tax due on inheritance.
You must create a Will. In case your circumstances change, The will, will ensure that the distribution of your assets and money are according to your desire. You can amend your Will if your ex-partner has moved in with another person after you have divorced. This Will render any Wills you may have made in the past invalid if you get married or form a civil partnership.

#### Characteristics of a will
A Will is a legal declaration that a testator intends to have his property carried out after his death. It contains a codicil as well as any writing that allows for a voluntary posthumous disposition. The will is a testamentary instrument that a person uses to make dispositions of his property after his death. It is revocable at any time during a person’s life. The testator can change a Will at any time. The executors are not required to produce it.

#### Two essential characteristics are mandatory for a Will:
1. It can come into effect only upon the death of the testator.
2. The testator must have the right to revoke it at any time.
Wills are useful to dispose of the property. However, they can also serve as executors or trustees for minor children. One important point is that the contents of the Will must state that it will be applied after the death of the executor. It cannot be considered a Will just by its heading. Section 63 of the Indian Succession Act 1925 states that a Will is revocable and can be altered when the maker can dispose of his property through Will.
If a person dies and does not make a Will, it is called “intestate”.  Therefore, His legal heirs will inherit his property by the law of inheritance. Notably, legal heirs are usually close family members like spouses, children, parents, and siblings. Most people want to be able to decide how they wish to dispose of their property. This is why it’s necessary to make one’s Will.

#### Hindu Law of Succession
According to the Hindu Law of Succession, relations can be divided into two types.
There are two classes: Class 1 and Class 2.
Class 1 legal heirs will have the first right to the property.
Class 2 legal inheritors will come into consideration if there is no Class 1 legal heir. Below is a table listing relations that fall under both Class 1 and 2.
Each one has equal rights to wealth.
Class 1 (First Priority).–Mother
Class 2 (will come into consideration if Class 1 is absent)–Father
(First Priority). Class 1–Widow (Wife or the deceased).
If Class 1 does not exist then next to kin –Brother/Sister
Class 1 (First Priority)–Son/Daughter
If Class 1 is absent, Class 2 will be considered.
Similarly in the absence of the legal heir the next to kin will be the rightful owner.

##### One share per category:
Each person will receive one share, and if more than one person is in each category, the share will be divided equally. If person C, who is entitled to share their wealth, had two children, A or B, both of whom are deceased, A had one child, and B had 2. For C, A and then B are the main legal inheritors. They are entitled to equal shares (50% each), but their children will now get it. A’s child will receive 50%, while B’s children will each get 25%. So B will split the 50% equally between his two children.
Your next step is easy. Look at your family to identify your legal heirs in the event that a will is not written. If you don’t make a will, they will receive a portion of your wealth. The division may not look perfect, but that doesn’t mean you should skip the Will. Get in touch with a lawyer in your area (or online) to prepare the Will and register it.

#### Contents of a will
It can be time and money saving include all important points in your Will. These are some of the things you should be thinking about:
How much immovable and movable property you have. This includes savings, occupational and private pensions, mutual funds investments, shares and property, insurance policies and bank and building society accounts.
Who do you want to benefit from your Will? Make a list of everyone you want to leave money or property. They are the beneficiaries. It is also wise to decide if you want to give any money to charity.
Who is responsible for looking after children younger than 18 years old?
Who will manage the estate and fulfil your wishes, as stated in the Will? These are the executors.

### Sale Agreement
An agreement to sell is a contract between two people in which one side agrees to sell the other thing at a later date. The transfer of ownership is done in exchange for part of a payment. Normally the sale agreement is done either when you purchase a new flat/villa/commercial space from a builder or from any individual for a resale property. You can use A registered instrument to transfer tangible immovable property above the value of Rs. 100.
Instead of an agreement to sell, Housing Boards and Development Authorities issue allotment letters.
Read the sale agreement carefully, before signing
It can take a lot of time to search for the right property. Finding your dream home can take many months or even years. After much negotiation, the agreement to sell must be signed with either the seller or builder. Once you have the agreement to sell (if you are buying a villa/flat from a builder, the builder will draft the agreement), make sure to read it before signing carefully.
After the agreement to sell is signed and stamped, the sale deed is prepared on the basis. The contract binds both parties. If one party fails to meet the commitments, the other party can cancel the contract. If you are not sure, read the documents carefully and don’t make any payments. Buyers often overlook important clauses that builders and sellers leave out of a sale agreement in their rush to complete paperwork. You should carefully read and comprehend each clause in sale agreements before you sign them. Otherwise, you could end up in a soup.
You should verify important information such as your name, address, father’s name., PAN and AAdhaar details. Also, confirm that the agreement lists the block number and flat number for the township.

#### Before signing an agreement review the following topics.
1. Right to modify specifications :
The company’s sales representative will show you a sample flat when you visit the construction site. A sample flat is a marketing tool that companies use to increase their sales. After the flats have been sold or the building is in possession, the builder destroys the models. Builders often include a clause that allows them to alter the layout plan of an apartment or flat showed to them. The final layout of your flat may look very different to the one you saw when you booked.
Sometimes the sample flat may appear larger than the actual one. If the agreement does not allow the builder to change the specifications, you will not bring the builder before a consumer court.
Smart Options
Remember to refer to the actual drawing as samples of flats are not intended. These are usually furnished with fixtures and furniture and may sometimes be slightly larger than an actual flat. The builder can’t change the plans once approved by the local Municipal Corporation or development authority. It would help if you insisted on the inclusion of the specific brand of fixtures in any agreement.
2. The area of the flat/house is measured in three ways:
Super area,
Built-up area,
Carpet area.
The super area also includes a proportionate amount of common facilities such as hallways, lobby, and staircases.
The apartment’s total area, including its walls, is called the built-up area. The actual area of the apartment is called the carpet area.
Builders should mention the carpet area in their agreement.
However, they almost always mention the super-built-up area, usually 20-30% larger than the carpet area.
What can you do?
Ask for the floor area. Look at the plans to get an idea of how big the apartment will be. Verify that the agreement’s carpet area is consistent with the builder’s promises.
3. Additional charges: A newspaper advertisement states that a 2BHK flat is available only for 255,000 It will cost you around 30 lakh to book the flat, friends. Advertisements do not reflect the actual property price. The buyer might have to pay 20-35% more. Flats that face a park or garden are typically more expensive than regular flats. A significant amount is spent on gym membership, car parking, common utilities, and maintenance fees.
Your point of action –
. Remember that developers won’t give you any free services. Get the builder to sign a list with all the fees. If there is something to hide, most developers will be careful about sharing any information in writing.
4 . Payment plan clause : the builder/seller makes the payment plan when you book. Check that the payment plan you have chosen is part of the agreement to sell. Also, ensure that all future payments and instalments to the builder are consistent with the plan. Before signing the agreement, you should read it with a magnifying glass!
You should check the date of payment to the builder, the amount to be paid, and the penalty clause. Builders usually include a penalty clause in the contract if you fail to pay the instalment on time. They charge 18% per annum. The agreement should mention if you are making an advance an advance to the builder.
What you can do
Keep in mind that a bank will only approve a loan for the amount you applied for. Before you buy, you should make an accurate estimate of your loan payment and margin agreement. Only purchase if you have the financial means to pay it. Visit the site to verify that the building’s construction stage is as per schedule before paying the instalment.
5 . Tax payment : you must pay the registration fees and service tax. It is the builders responsibility to pay the property tax before handing over possession.
Your option
Before signing any agreement with the builder you must resolve the issue. You must ensure the builder pays the taxes.
6. Is there an escalation clause
Avoid projects that have an escalation clause. In the builder-buyer contract, builders often include a cost-escalation clause. This clause states that the developer can raise the project’s cost if materials or other inputs become more expensive. This clause is usually a part of the application form of state housing boards. You don’t need to worry about them as the state government manages them.
Ask about the builder’s experience. Has he ever implemented an escalation clause? Is there a legitimate reason he would implement the clause? You can also choose a developer that does not include an “escalation” clause in the sale agreement.
7. What date will the project finish? Or when is the project due for completion?
Developers usually build projects phase-wise so they can meet their time commitment. This period usually lasts between 36 and 42 months from the date of construction. If the contract does not specify a starting date, you can email the developer for a written response. Developers will usually give an approximate timeframe within which they will transfer the property ownership to the buyer.
It is important that the builder clearly states the month and year of possession. The buyer’s best interest is to include a clause in the purchase agreement that mentions the date the developer will give over possession.
8 . Inclusion of penalty clause in case of any delays
You can include the penalty the builder must pay for delaying payment. If the developer delays in giving possession to the buyer, he will pay the penalty. Please find out the amount of penalty he will have to pay. What penalty did he pay for past delays? Or did he find a way around it? In case of any delays in the project, talk Talk to the builder. You can also contact the media or consumer court if he doesn’t listen.
9 . Assignment or transfer: This clause does not apply if you’re buying a flat for a residential purpose. This clause is important if you’re investing in a flat. Builders usually mention this clause to limit your ability to transfer your property to another person until forming of the housing society. It may prove not easy to sell your property before the formation of the housing society. To sell your property, you might need a societys no-objection certificate.
It is best to avoid transferring property to a third party before forming of the society. This is because it is easier for members to deal with properties already created and shares certificates issued. Legally, however, a builder can’t ask you to pay the penalty if the property is transferred to another person.



## Art of Negotiating

Nowadays, the supply of real estate is greater than the demand .
There are many ways to bargain when purchasing a property. However, before you buy a property, it is important to do your research and learn the following facts:
How much are property prices in the area you plan to buy?
What is the reputation of the builder in the marketplace?
How many years has the company been in real estate transactions?
What facilities are available in this particular area?
How much does it cost to pre-launch a project?
Why are the flats being sold at a higher price by the builder?
Learn about the influencing factors that may have contributed to price appreciation.
You can bargain more to get a better deal, but it is important to remember the negative aspects of the property. This may be a plus for bargaining.
Most builders will accept a reduction in their property prices but not necessarily declare it.

#### Factors that affect bargaining
1. Builder/developer’s market reputation – An established builder will sell his project at the launching stage, so there is no room for bargaining.
2. In the event of a recession, you will have more bargaining power if the market/economy is in good condition.
3. It is important to determine whether the developer used his own money or borrowed money from a bank to develop the project. He can keep the property if he uses his own money and may not be willing to negotiate. He would want to sell the project quickly if he borrowed money from a bank. In this case, he may be more open to negotiations as interest rates will continue to increase the construction cost. It is important to determine if the land is owned, leased, or purchased.
People don’t do enough research before buying. They also don’t spend enough time researching the developer and his past projects. They are not able to negotiate well because they lack research.

##### Art of Bargaining
Pay attention and take the time to verify facts. Do not speak up during negotiations. Take notes and make sure to give a realistic rate. Keep a copy of your sanction letter and a chequebook handy, so developers don’t underestimate you.
Opportunity
1. Obtain a provisional letter of sanction from your bank. This will prove to developers that you are serious about business.
2. Make a group of property buyers. This group could include your family members, colleagues, or friends. Developers prefer group bookings because they can sell more flats with one deal, and members get a good discount.
3. Decide your budget.
4. Take a look at the area where you plan to buy the property. Ask people living in the area to determine the current market price of the property. You can bargain with the builder if you discover that many flats are vacant because of a slump in the market.
5. Property fairs and property carnivals are great places to get amazing discounts. These are the best places to look at all properties in your area.
Bargaining Procedure
Let’s say you decide to buy a flat in a specific apartment. You are now sitting across the table of the builder. With a chequebook in your hand, you ask the builder to give you his “best price” (maximum price for the flat). It would help to quote a realistic but lower price to the builder when he quotes the minimum price.
Offer a realistic price when you are bargaining. Don’t offer a price that is impossible for the builder. After you have presented a lower but realistic price, you can tell the builder that you will reconsider.
Keep your options open, as you want to buy the property and not defeat the builder by bargaining. Be flexible and Accept the offer if you are happy with it. If you and your family love the flat and can afford it, don’t hesitate to accept the offer. Take a step back, analyze the situation, and then work according to it.

They help to verify the quality of a project.  Eight principles are the basis of the International Organization for Standardization’s certification for quality management systems. These include customer satisfaction and customer focus. A certified ISO 9001:2008 builder who is under audit is more professional.

#### Consider your family size before booking an apartment.

If you are unmarried, think about the possibilities and situations that may arise after marriage. Prepare and plan accordingly before investing. If you have booked 2 BHK as a bachelor, you may want three BHK post marriage. With time and in different stages of life, perspectives and wants change.
It is also possible that a newly married couple may invest in a studio apartment. They may not realise they will need more space later on when their children are born and their parents move in with them.  Separate study rooms may be required for the children as they grow up.
Do some basic calculations if you’re thinking about buying or already planning to buy a dream home. This could be a small home, a large home, a luxury home, a villa, or even a farm home. Most importantly, You should calculate the size of your family and how many children you have, or what the expected or desired number of children you will have in the future.

## Capital Gains

**URL:** https://www.prospertyrealestate.com/capital-gains/

**Words:** 2226


### What is Capital Gain?
During the selling of a property, A term called “Capital Gain” come into the picture. It’s a word that brings happiness and a lot of tension. Capital gains, which are profits or gains from the transfer or disposition of capital assets in the preceding year, will be subject to income tax under the heading Capital Gains unless IT is exempted u/s 54B, 54D or 54E, 54F,54EC, 54F or 54G.

#### Capital Asset:
This is an important part of Capital Gain. Section 2(14), Income-tax Act 1961, defines a capital asset as any property that an assessee holds, regardless of association with his business or profession. However, certain items such as a consumable store, stock in trade, or raw materials used for his own business, profession, personal effects, and certain agricultural lands do not count. Capital gains would not apply to jewellery that is for personal use. Capital assets include house, flat, penthouse and farmhouse as well as shops/showrooms and land.

#### Transfer of Capital Asset:
Capital gains are only possible with a transfer of a capital asset. The transfer of capital assets for capital gains is described as sale, exchange, relinquishment, or even an asset’s disposition. Section 5 of 1882’s Transfer of Property Act defines property transfer as the act by which a living individual transfers property to one or more living persons. To transfer property means to do such an act. If the sale price exceeds the market rate, then the sale consideration is subject to stamp duty and capital gain purposes.
Example –
Suppose the sale price is lower than the circle rate, the circle rate on the property being sold will be the deemed sale amount. Section 50C of Income-tax Act 1961 clearly states that the value of a transaction adopted for stamp duty purposes would be the deemed sale consideration for the property to calculate the capital gains due to an assessee. Suppose the assessee claims that the Stamp Valuation Authority or Sub Registrar Office exceeds the property’s fair market value as the sale deed execution. In that case, the Assessing Officer can refer the property to a Valuation Officer.
The transfer would be the date that possession of the immovable property is given to the other party. It will be a sale transaction if there is an agreement between two parties. If payment has been received, but the transfer has not been affected, or the sale deed has not been executed, it will not be considered a sale transaction. The income tax rule clarifies that income tax will only be charged under the head capital gains if a transfer of capital assets in the preceding year. No transfer means no capital gain.

#### Short-term Capital Gain :
The income earned from the immovable property is a short-term capital gain if it is sold within 36 months of the purchase date. Tax savings are not possible for short-term capital gains. Short-term capital gains would result in a tax liability. This could be calculated by adding short-term capital gains to other taxable income for the year and then calculating the tax liability according to the tax rates on total income.

#### Capital Gain for Long-Term :
A capital gain derived from a property held for more than 36 months from the date of purchase, sale or deed is a long-term capital gain. Taxes on long-term capital gains are at 20%. Cost Inflation Index is the chart to calculate the amount of capital gains.
This was when the asset was in possession from the date of purchase to the date the owner sold it. It is the most important factor in determining whether the asset is a short-term capital gain or long-term.

#### Capital Gain Bonds
They are financial instruments that are for investors with long-term capital gain tax liability. REC or PFC can issue these bonds, but they also come in NHAI. This category does not include any other bonds. All investors can eliminate their capital gains tax liability by investing capital gains bonds according to section 54EC under the Income-tax Act of 1961. The net long-term capital gains must be invested in bonds within six months after selling the property.
The lock-in period is for at least five years, w.e.f. 01.04.2020. These bonds allow you to invest a maximum of 50 lakh per financial year. This means that a taxpayer can only invest a limited amount.
Alternative options include investing in residential property for individuals/HUFs in certain situations to avoid capital gains tax. You can carry forward any capital loss, short-term or long-term, and use it for the next eight years.

#### Capital Gains Tax
Capital gain tax is the name  of the tax that you pay on capital gains. Tax @ 20% is charged on long-term capital gains. Selling a residential property, plot, or other assets may result in long-term capital gains.
Section 54F of the 1961 Income Tax Act states that if someone sells capital assets other than a residential house, they can save capital gain taxes by investing all the consideration in a new residential home.

##### However, the law does not allow for certain conditions.
The condition is that the new residential property must be purchased or constructed within three years of acquisition. If you sell the property before the three years, then the acquisition cost shall be subtracted from the amount of capital gain exempted under section 54 earlier. This transfer will result in a capital gain that is short-term capital gain.
Section 54 of 1961’s Income Tax Act states that Mr X should only purchase property in his name if he wants to avoid capital gains tax. He would not be eligible to save capital gains tax if he bought the property in the name of his spouse or any other relatives. This is a fact you should not forget. This principle applies if you wish to build a residential house on a plot, not in your name or if you plan to buy a vacant plot to build thereon.
Capital gain bonds are only suitable for people who are willing to take some risk in their lives. Capital bonds are not advisable to generate a 12-15 % return through investing in real estate, businesses, and the share market.
So, All individuals are exempt from the Assessment Year 1999-2000 HUF for one residential property and any vacant land with a maximum area of 500 sq. m under Section 5 (vi), 1957 Wealth Tax Act.

#### Cost Inflation Index (CII).
The cost inflation index can help you save enough tax on long-term capital gains. This index applies only to long-term capital gains and not for short-term capital gains.

##### How to calculate?
A set formula is there to calculate long-term capital gains.
First, determine the financial year when you purchased the house. Write down the cost of purchasing/acquisition (purchasing price including registration charge and brokerage charge) and cost of inflation index for that year. Write down the cost inflation index for the year that you sold the asset. Now multiply the acquisition cost by the cost inflation of the year of asset transfer or sale. Divide the result by the cost inflation for the year of the asset acquisition/purchase
If the purchase of the house were before 01/04/2001, then its fair market value would be the acquisition cost of the property. The cost inflation index for that asset would be used to determine the cost inflation of 2001-02, or it should be considered that the property was purchased on the 1st of April 2001.
* You can purchase a plot of land and then build a house. Sometimes you buy a one-storey house, then later build another. This means that any costs incurred for improving an asset must be adjusted similarly using the cost inflation index.
First, by multiplying the cost of improvement with the cost inflation index of the year, the asset is transferred. Then this is divided by the cost inflation index for the year when improvement/construction to the asset was made.

##### The Government of India publishes the cost inflation index each year.
